- 1 - 
個股報告

毅嘉(2402) 
光通產品高速增長，海外產能持續擴張 
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 $71 
前次投資建議：無

報告日期：2026.10.27 研 究 員：邱岳霖

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q4 光通訊量產，惟缺料與淡季影響營運動能，EPS 0.41元。 
➢ AI 光通訊放量帶動營收倍增，mSAP量產為毛利率改善關鍵，2027 年 EPS 3.1 元，考量公
司切入高速成長之光通賽道，未來幾年營運將高速增長，兆豐投顧給予目標價 71 元(23X 
27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 23.9%，投資評等為「逢低買進」 。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/10/22 收盤價： 57.30 ➢ 光通訊散熱型金屬外殼已於26Q3量產 ，FlipChip打件與
液冷測漏板於26Q4導入量產，預期光通訊營收佔比由
1%升至6%，然MLCC、DSP、PCB三項缺料影響，同
時進入消費性產品淡季，預估毅嘉 26Q4營收32.7億
元，QoQ -4.3%，毛利率14.5%，季增0.2ppt，稅後淨
利1.3億元，QoQ +23.9%，EPS 0.41元。 
➢ 展望後市，AI光通訊將成為毅嘉主要成長動能，預期AI
相關營收佔比將由26Q4之6%躍升至2027年40%， 係因
800G光收發模組需求持續強勁，目前主要客戶明年總
需求為27-30KK，此外1.6T/3.2T世代將於2028至2029
年接續拉升，帶動Flip Chip打件需求大幅擴大，而毅嘉
持續在馬來西亞擴產，雖帶動營收顯著提升，不過因採
turnkey出貨，料件佔BOM cost約80%至90%，故毛利
率僅約5%至8%，將稀釋整體毛利率。後續改善關鍵在
於mSAP高密度電路板，目前已啟動客戶樣品打樣，預
期27Q3量產，毛利率可達40%以上；此外散熱事業營
收倍增並轉虧為盈，攜手杰華精密切入光模組散熱外
殼，加上IMS延伸至儲能與機器人應用，將使產品組合
逐步轉佳。綜上所述，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉 2027年營收
248.9億元，YoY +94.9%，毛利率11.5%，年減2.5ppt，
稅後淨利9.5億元，YoY +159%，EPS 3.1元。考量公
司切入高速成長之光通賽道 ，未來幾年營運將高速增
長，兆豐投顧給予目標價71元(23X 27(F)PER) ，潛在
上漲空間23.9%，投資評等為「逢低買進」 。

- 2 - 
個股報告 
資料來源：CMoney；兆豐投顧預估

營 運 分 析

毅嘉科技為軟板及機構整合廠  
毅嘉科技成立於 1983 年，初期以生產手機按鍵為主，於 2000 年掛牌上市
並跨足軟板製造領域，2020 年延伸至硬板打件並在跨入車用模組，2024 年
將戰略重心擴張至光通訊領域，從早期供應光收發模組用軟硬結合板，2026
年切入光收發模組 Flip Chip SMT，於 26Q3 完成認證並於 26Q4 放量生產，
預計 2027 年再導入 mSAP 高密度電路板，預期 27Q3 開始量產，此外毅嘉
於 26Q2 投資光通訊零組件廠商杰華精密(持股 46.25%)跨入光模組金屬外
殼。

毅嘉現主營軟板及機構整合業務：26Q3 應用別營收組成為車用 62%(智能座
艙 40%，連接板 19%，內飾件 3%)，其他消費 10%，遊戲 9%，穿戴設備
8%，其他產品 7%，手機 3%，光通訊 1%(26Q4 預估升至 6%)。生產基地
位於台灣林口廠(研發中心、散熱模組、IMS 研發中心)、大陸蘇州廠(車用軟
板、空板、 SMT、Mini LED)、大陸中山廠(穿戴裝置)、馬來西亞檳城及吉
打廠(25Q3 完工，產品為機構件、光通訊、車用、儲能 )，越南及匈牙利設
有銷售據點，北美據點評估中(德州)。

- 3 - 
個股報告 
26Q3 營收創今年新高，然新廠成本與匯損壓抑獲利，EPS 0.33元 
毅嘉(2402)公告 26Q3財報：營收 34.2 億元，QoQ +4.4%/YoY +18.4%，再
創近年新高，主要受惠車用及消費性電子產品需求成長帶動，毛利率 14.3%，
季增 0.1ppt，然因馬來西亞新廠初期營運成本與費用增加，營業利益較上季
衰退 10.5%，同時受人民幣升值產生匯兌損失，故稅後淨利 1 億元，QoQ 
-16.0%/YoY -45.1%，EPS 0.33元。

26Q4 光通訊量產，惟缺料與淡季影響營運動能，EPS 0.41元 
光通訊散熱型金屬外殼已於 26Q3 量產，FlipChip打件與液冷測漏板於 26Q4
導入量產，預期光通訊營收佔比由 1%升至 6%，然 MLCC、DSP、PCB 三
項缺料影響，同時進入消費性產品淡季，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉 26Q4 營收 32.7
億元，QoQ -4.3%/YoY +20.0%，毛利率 14.5%，季增 0.2ppt，年減 5.3ppt，
稅後淨利 1.3 億元，QoQ +23.9%/YoY -32.9%，EPS 0.41元。

AI 光通訊放量帶動營收倍增，mSAP量產為毛利率改善關鍵，2027年 EPS 
3.1元 
展望後市，AI 光通訊將成為毅嘉主要成長動能，預期 AI 相關營收佔比將由
26Q4之 6%躍升至 2027 年 40%，係因 800G 光收發模組需求持續強勁，目
前主要客戶明年總需求為 27-30KK，此外 1.6T/3.2T 世代將於 2028 至 2029
年接續拉升，帶動 Flip Chip 打件需求大幅擴大，而毅嘉持續在馬來西亞擴
產，Flip Chip 產線將由今年 3條提升至 2027 年 8-10條，2028 年進一步擴
充至 24條，雖帶動營收顯著提升，不過因採 turnkey 出貨，料件佔 BOM cost
約 80%至 90%，故毛利率僅約 5%至 8%，將稀釋整體毛利率。後續改善關
鍵在於 mSAP高密度電路板，目前已啟動客戶樣品打樣，預期 27Q3量產，
毛利率可達 40%以上；此外散熱事業營收倍增並轉虧為盈，攜手杰華精密切
入光模組散熱外殼，加上IMS 延伸至儲能與機器人應用，將使產品組合逐步
轉佳。綜上所述，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉2027 年營收 248.9 億元，YoY +94.9%，
毛利率 11.5%，年減2.5ppt，稅後淨利9.5 億元，YoY +159%，EPS 3.1 元。

Poya  (5904.TWO)
Strong 3Q as Expected; Tougher Comparisons Ahead
CITI'S TAKE 
Poya’s 3Q26 net profit +26% y/y came largely in-line with our forecast, and 
5% ahead of BBG consensus. SSSG of 7.0%, higher GPM, opex savings and 
stronger op leverage lifted OPM to 17.5%, the key drivers to strong 
profitability. Looking into 4Q, tougher comp should moderate sales and 
earnings growth despite fast store additions and solid operation execution. 
We maintain Neutral as the strong share price rally YTD appears to reflect 
most positives from robust SSSG and margin expansion. A higher comp 
base and potentially intensifying competition limit the room for further re-
rating. 
3Q26 broadly in-line; solid margins boosted by strong op leverage — Poya 
reported 3Q26 net profits of NT$1.0bn +26% y/y, largely in-line with our est and 5% 
above BBG’s est. 3Q sales of NT$7.4bn +15% y/y were supported by solid SSSG 
+7.0% y/y with ticket size +4.9% and traffic +2.0%. Higher GPM, strong opex 
savings and op leverage lifted OPM to 17.5%, topping our/BBG est of 17.1%/16.6%. 
T otal store count reached 495 at end-3Q26 (+25 net increase in 9M26). 
4Q outlook: faster store expansion meets a higher comp base — We expect 30 net 
store additions in 4Q26E vs. 35 stores prior, bringing the year-end store count to 
525 and supporting continued sales growth. Demand for beauty-related products 
remains healthy. However, the elevated comparison base from 4Q26E onward 
should moderate SSSG and overall sales and earnings growth, despite the 
contribution from new stores. We believe this potential growth deceleration could 
temper investor enthusiasm and trigger profit-taking pressure.
Implications: Slightly positive reaction expected; maintain Neutral — We keep our 
earnings estimates for 26-28E largely unchanged in view of the in-line 3Q results. 
We expect a slightly positive market reaction to the results given the healthy margin 
expansion. However, strong sales had likely raised expectations for 3Q profitability, 
limiting the scope for a more meaningful positive surprise. Our Neutral thesis 
remains unchanged – we believe the strong share price rally has priced in much of 
the benefit from robust SSSG and margin improvement. Looking ahead, tougher 
comp could constrain growth, while potentially intensifying competition, including 
Cosmed’s introduction of Olive Young products, could challenge Poya’s beauty-led 
momentum. We therefore see limited room for further re-rating despite solid 
operating execution. 
n
Angela Hsu AC

### Page 5

Poya  (5904.TWO)
27 October 2026 Citi Research
5
Poya
Company description
Poya is the largest retailer of fast-moving consumer goods in Taiwan, focusing
on low-priced cosmetics, accessories, clothing and food products for females
aged 15-49. Poya had 470 stores in Taiwan at the end of 2025 and believes
that the market can accommodate up to 700-800 stores before reaching
saturation.
Investment strategy
We have a Neutral rating on Poya. We think YTD rally has baked in most

positives, including strong SSSG and solid margin expansion. While
fundamentals remain solid, tougher comparisons from 4Q26E could
moderate this year's 6-7% SSSG, lowering operating leverage and capping
further earnings upside. Meanwhile, Cosmed's expanding rollout of Olive
Young brands is intensifying competition for beauty spending. Although
Poya's SSSG has remained resilient, braoder distribution of these brands
could divert customer traffic and increase promotional pressure. We continue

to recognize Poya's execution in store expansion and ongoing efficiency
improvements. However, with these strengths largely reflected in the share
price and growth momentum facing a higher hurdle from 4Q26E onward, we
see a more balanced risk/reward.
Valuation
We derive our 12-month target price of NT$70 for Poya from a DCF valuation
method. Our assumptions include a terminal growth rate of 4.5% and WACC
of 10.1% (beta of 1.05; risk-free rate of 4%; risk premium of 7.0%; cost of equity

of 11.3%). Our target price implies a P/E of 18x on our NTM EPS.
Risks
Key downside risks that could mean the shares fail to achieve our target price
include: intense price competition among online retailers, slower store traffic,
margin dilution due to aggressive store expansion, and a soft macro
environment.
Key upside risks that could mean the shares fail to achieve our target price
include: easing price competition among online retailers, stronger store

traffic, margin expansion thanks to strong cost control, and a stronger macro
environment.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.

after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

Update
Hon Precision  | Asia Pacific
CPO  handler on track for 1Q27
mass production; stay OW
Key takeaways from yesterday's call:
• CPO/CPC: Has shipped  a small volume of  Insertion 4 OE CPO FT
qualification handlers in 4Q26, which are on track for mass production in
1Q27. Management expects qualification to start in November and end before
the year-end. It's also working on a CPC handler project with Japanese ATE
and a US customer who showed strong interest.

• Mega Tray Handler: Around 20-30% of customer bookings are Mega Trays,
while full system ASP would increase by at least 30% from original handlers.
Has also shipped the handler for customer qualification.
• Preliminary 2027 outlook:  To see strong revenue growth  in 2H27 from
Mega Trays and CPO-related demand as these higher ASP solutions will start
mass production in 1H27, with revenue recognition typically taking 3-6
months.
• Capacity outlook: 2027 remains on a 50% Y/Y pace, while the equipment

unit growth might be lower than 50% given the higher complexity of new
equipment.
Reiterate OW: Hon Precision is a key beneficiary of the expanding AI testing TAM.
We see the recent pullback as an attractive entry point, with the stock now trading
at 24x 2027e EPS vs. a 2025–28e earnings CAGR of >70%.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hon Precision (7769.TW)
We derive our price target from a residual income model, as we believe this can best capture
the long-term value. We assume a 9.8% cost of equity (risk-free rate 2.0% and risk premium
6.0%), a payout ratio of 65%, a medium-term growth rate of 15%, and a terminal growth rate
of 4.0%. Our price target implies 77x 2026e, 45x 2027e and 27x 2028e P/Es.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 142: 261029_ms_vanguard-DG

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_ms_vanguard-DG.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29

### Page 1

M
Idea
VSMC could cause  high-single digit (%) dilution to 2027 GM – above many
investors’ 3-5% expectation: On Vanguard’s latest earnings call, management noted
a successful trial run of VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, its JV
with NXP Semiconductors. It confirmed that interposers are on track for mass
production  in 1Q27. We see revenue upside from the ramp – but depreciation could
heavily pressure margins. Management previously guided that VSMC could break

even at 30kwpm, more likely in late 2028, in our view (vs. current 8-inch margin at
30-35%). VSMC  capacity is scheduled at 44kwpm, ~100kwpm 8-inch equivalent,
about one-third of current 8-inch capacity (280kwpm). On the other hand, we think
new capacity may only run at <5kwpm in 1Q27 and <20kwpm by end-2027;
depreciation reflects 44kwpm (VSMC's full capacity) for infrastructure, clean room,
and construction. We therefore project that VSMC's gross margin dilution in full-

year 2027 could be more severe than many expect.
Despite strong customer commitment, VSMC could still dilute the corporate
margin average for at least the next five years: Since management previously
guided that VSMC could break even at 30kwpm, we view its break-even point as late
2028 or 1H29. It will then take years for GM to rise from 0% to 30-40%, even if the
capacity is fully booked. We thus expect a drag on corporate average GM for at least

the next five years. The good news: VSMC demand is skewed to high-margin
businesses –  interposers, industrial, and automotive – so the margin improvement
could be smoother  vs. peers like Hua Hong.
2027 might bring double-digit wafer price hikes and AI-related revenue at 20-
30% of the mix: During its latest earnings call, UMC guided for a 10% blended
wafer ASP increase in 2027. Given larger exposure to AI server PMIC, industrial, and
automotive, we expect Vanguard to raise 8-inch wafer prices 10-15% for the full year.

Given some customer pushback to front-loaded hikes, the price hike might be split –
twice a year, each 5-10%. Also, the company would still have L TAs expiring in 2027,
so we don’t expect aggressive wafer ASP guidance for 1Q27. With new interposer
production, we believe AI-related revenue could reach 20-30% of total revenue.
Down to UW from EW: The margin impact is larger than we initially expected and
could last 5-10 years.  Our new price target implies 2.8x our 2027e BVPS.

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Vanguard could still show strong revenue/margin
performance for 2H26
4Q26 should maintain strong growth: We expect Vanguard to sustain strong
performance into 4Q26, supported by resilient demand from the industrial and
automotive end markets. In addition to a favorable demand outlook, ongoing capacity
optimization initiatives, particularly the transition from thicker-wire to thinner-wire
manufacturing processes, should enhance production efficiency, improve capacity

utilization, and support higher output. These operational improvements, coupled with a
favorable product mix and steady order momentum, could further underpin revenue
growth and profitability in 4Q26. We now expect Vanguard 8-inch process margin to reach
34-36% in both 4Q26 and 1Q27.

Key questions to ask during the upcoming earnings call on November 6:
1.  Price hike into 2027: How much of the incremental cost could Vanguard pass
through  to customers? Could we see double-digit apples-to-apples price hikes into
2027 despite the expiration of some L TAs?

2.  AI-related revenue: How much could  AI related revenue grow in 2027 if we
include both AI server PMIC and interposer production?
3.  Singapore fab – margin dilution and capacity allocation: How could  margin be
affected by VSMC in both 2027 and 2028? How much would depreciation be in
2027 and 2028? How would the company allocate 12-inch capacity between
interposers, NXP, and other customers?
4.  Consumer demand: Has the company seen any slowdown in consumer demand?

And how soon could that capacity be filled up by other sources of demand?
5.  Further capacity expansion: Might the company have further plans to expand 8-
inch capacity or second Singapore fab since the utilization rate has been reaching
>90%?

### Page 6

M
Idea
6
Valuation  methodology
We lower our price target (base case value) to NT$135 from NT$180:  We factor in our
earnings estimate changes for 2027-28 and also lower the intermediate growth rate
assumption from 13% to 10.5% to reflect the long-term margin dilution.
We continue to derive our price target from a residual income model, which we believe
best captures the stock's long-term value. Our key assumptions other than the
intermediate growth rate are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2,

equity risk premium of 6% and risk-free rate of 2%),  and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.
We also lower our bull and bear case values from NT$260 and NT$110 to NT$195 and NT
$80, accordingly, implying 4.0x and 1.7x our 2027e BVPS.

MS Rating
▪ We expect 2026 capex to increase beyond
2025's high level given Singapore fab

expansion, leading to long-term margin
pressure.
▪ We expect PMIC wafer price hikes in the
industry in response to strong demand from
AI. Vanguard could beneﬁt from TSMC
mature node consolidation as well as
interposer production.
▪ The new Singapore fab could cause high-
single-digit margin dilution to the corporate
average in 2027.
▪ VSMC's 12-inch capacity is all fully booked;
production will start to ramp in 2027 with

Risk Reward Themes

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here
NT$195.00
4.0x base case 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi demand: Auto semi demand is
exceptionally strong; inventory is digested
quickly, and DDI for smartphones and large

panels shows robust growth. We forecast a
>30% revenue CAGR in 2025-28.
Competition from peers eases. We assume:
1) global semi sales growth of >20% and
foundry industry growth of >15% in 2026; 2)
better-than-expected demand for 8"
capacity; and 3) power demand for 5G
tracking ahead of schedule.
NT$135.00
2.8x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth slowdown: Demand has
started to pick up for LDDIC and PMIC,
while auto semis inventory digestion is
reaching an end. We forecast a 25% revenue

CAGR in 2025-28. Vanguard starts to
reverse pricing discounts to customers in
view of stronger demand and some rush
orders in the short run. We assume: 1) global
semi sales show growth in 2026; and 2) the
foundry industry shows single-digit growth
in 2026.
NT$80.00
1.7x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth decelerates: Auto semi demand
weakens markedly; power semi inventory
levels remain elevated. Smartphone DDI
shows a worse-than-expected decline.
Intensiﬁed competition and new Chinese

capacity dampen the overall pricing
environment. We forecast <10% revenue
CAGR in 2025-28. We assume: 1) overall
semi industry continues to decelerate in

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

8422 可寧衛(Cleanaway)

報告日期： 2026/10/22 研 究 員：洪  國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 高雄 205 兵工廠專案整治案，己於 2024~2025 年分別認列營
收金額 2 億元與 12 億元，初期主要以開挖為主，掩埋次之，
預期進入中後期掩埋高峰階段，預估 2026~2027 年營收金額
10 億元與 10 億元。  
 小港焚化新爐興建，在 BOT 會計制度，總興建成本 350 億元，
工程期間 3.5 年，在興建期間需認列工程成本當作營收認列，
毛利率為零 ， 宏遠推估新爐2026 年貢獻營收金額 25 億元 ，2027
年貢獻營收金額 40 億元。  
一. 預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 0.33 元 
在 BOT 興建工程認列金額較 2Q26 減少因素，可寧衛 3Q26 營收
金額 19.89 億元 ，YoY+88.45% ，QoQ-12.29% ， 預估營業利益5.28
億元， YoY+44.72% ，QoQ-4.47% ，預估稅後盈餘 4.46 億元，
YoY+58.37% ，QoQ-2.79% ，預估稅後 EPS 0.33 元。  
二. 預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 0.37 元 
在 4Q26 興建工程進入高峰，預估可寧衛 4Q26 營收金額 22.09
億元， YoY+122.16% ，QoQ+11.04% ，預估營業利益 6.23 億元，
YoY+78.56% ， QoQ+17.95% ，預估稅後盈餘 5.09 億元，
YoY+82.15% ，QoQ+14.12% ，預估稅後 EPS 0.37 元。  
三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.25 元 
可寧衛 2026 年雖然在 205 兵工廠整治工程認列金額減少 ， 但2026
年 205 兵工廠營收認列主要以高毛利掩埋比重為主，帶動整體獲
利呈現小幅成長。預估可寧衛 2026 年營收金額 75.80 億元，
YoY+58.99% ，預估營業利益 20.87 億元， YoY+7.54% ，預估稅
後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.25 元。  
四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 1.35 元 
可寧衛 2027 年在 205 兵工廠掩埋處理量小幅增加，加上大承再
生粒料廠貢獻全年度營運，預期可寧衛 2027 年營運將持續成長。
宏遠預估可寧衛 2027 年營收金額 96.00 億元， YoY+26.65% ，預
估營業利益 22.97 億元 ，YoY+10.08% ， 預估稅後盈餘18.35 億元 ，
YoY+8.24% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.35 元。  
五. 投資建議買進，目標價 34 元 
觀察可寧衛歷史 PER11~30X ，2026 年 ROE 16.9% ，PER 21X ，
2027 年 ROE17% ，PER 19.3X 。評價偏低，故投資建議買進，目
標價 34 元（ 25X2027 年稅後 EPS ）。

2

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
可寧衛成立於 1999 年，經營有害事業廢棄物之處理業務，資本額
13.59 億元，主要業務包含有害重金屬污泥廢棄物固化清理、鋼鐵
業集塵灰固化清理、都市垃圾焚化爐飛灰固化清理、有害石綿廢
棄物固化清理與非法棄置場址整治業務，可寧衛為全國最大一站
式開挖、清運、固化、掩埋垂直整合之合法環保公司。可寧衛旗
下有五間子公司，其中岡聯事業以廢棄物清運為主、大倉實業以
一般事業廢棄物掩埋場、可寧衛企業以固化物專屬掩埋場、吉衛
以固化物專屬掩埋場、可寧衛投資主要負責中國大陸業務為主。

圖一、可寧衛集團

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二. 現行廢棄物處理方式與市場概況  
廢棄物主要分成一般廢棄物與事業廢棄物，一般廢棄物主要以家
庭垃圾為主，在經過分類回收後，將進行焚化再中間處理，其中
可寧衛為最終掩埋廠。事業廢棄物在經過資源回收，再區分成有
害廢棄物與一般事業廢棄物，經過焚化處理後，再進行中間處理
（固化處理），最終進入掩埋。可寧衛為最終的固化處理與掩埋。

三.可寧衛公司經營模式與客戶概況  
可寧衛主要客戶分兩大類，第一類為製程客戶，如鋼鐵廠、焚化
爐、電子公司、汙水處理廠等，生產過程中排放出來的廢棄物，
如汞、鋅、銅或其他金屬，首先交由岡聯事業清運，分出一般廢
棄物與有害廢棄，如果是一般廢棄物由大倉事業負責掩埋，若是
有害廢棄物則先做固化處理 ， 再交由可寧衛企業/吉衛來負責掩埋 。

4

第二類客戶為汙染清理客戶，主要處理非法棄置場址與關廠及土
水法列場址，可寧衛歷年來在關廠廠址業務有新竹飛利浦竹北
廠、桃園日商太平洋玻璃廠、台塑前鎮廠、南亞林園 DOP 廠、中
石化前鎮廠、台電興邦獅甲段、台肥前鎮廠、高雄榮民工程等。

圖四、可寧衛服務流程

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

四. 2026 年營運概況及 2027 年展望  
營收來源主要分成製程客戶與汙染清理客戶。在製程客戶營收淡
旺季主要需視客戶淡旺季而定， 1Q 在工作天數與下游客戶出貨量
較少情況下，為傳統淡季，之後逐季走高至 3Q，而後 4Q 將會略
為下滑。在汙染清理客戶方面，主要視案量大小與時間長短而定，
無明顯淡旺季之分。

可寧衛營收結構包括廢棄物固化及開挖、廢棄物掩埋、廢棄物清
運服務、再生紙銷售、預拌混凝土銷售、太陽能發電與其他共七
項。可寧衛 2025 年營收概況，廢棄物固化及開挖 2025 年營收金
額 17.71 億元， YoY-18.7% ；廢棄物掩埋 2025 年營收金額 15.8
億元 ，YoY-10.0% ； 廢棄物清運服務2025 年營收金額 1.44 億元，
YoY+37.1% ；再生紙銷售 2025 年營收金額 4.30 億元，
YoY-35.0% ；預拌混凝土銷售 2025 年營收金額 2.39 億元，
YoY+131.6% ；太陽能發電 2025 年營收金額 5.75 億元，
YoY+63.1% 。

廢棄物固化及開挖 2025 年營收金額 17.71 億元， YoY-18.7% 。觀
察營收衰退主因在於高雄中油煉油廠專案整治工程帶動
2023~2024 年廢棄物固化及開挖營收維持在相對高檔水準。 2025
年僅有 205 兵工廠專案，且開挖與固化比重相對較少因素。預期
2026 年，在 205 兵工廠專案整治工程減少，加上國內石綿瓦專案
固化金額未能彌補 205 兵工廠專案 ， 預估廢棄物固化及開挖2026
年營收金額 8.20 億元， YoY-53.7% 。預期 2027 年，在石綿瓦專
案逐漸結束，固化處理量減少，預估廢棄物固化及開挖 2027 年營
收金額 7.50 億元， YoY-8.5% 。

廢棄物掩埋服務 2025 年營收金額 15.8 億元， YoY-10.0% ，觀察
衰退主因在於 2025 年在 205 兵工廠專案，掩埋量相較高雄中油
專案來的低。預期 2026 年，在製程客戶掩埋量小幅增加，但在
205 兵工廠專案掩埋金額小幅增加 ， 預估廢棄物掩埋2026 年營收
金額 26.00 億元， YoY+64.6% 。預期 2027 年，在 205 兵工廠持
續 ， 掩埋量可望微幅增加 ， 預估廢棄物掩埋2027 年營收金額 26.50
億元， YoY+1.90% 。

再生紙銷售主要以上評資源為主。上評資源主要產品為各類牛皮
紙，瓦楞芯紙，工業管用紙，特殊用紙及植生資材之製造及分條
加工。再生紙銷售 2025 年營收金額 4.3 億元， YoY-35.0% 。預估
再生紙銷售 2026 年營收金額 1.7 億元， YoY-60.5% 。預計 4Q26
結束再生紙業務， 2027 年無營收貢獻。

預拌混凝土銷售主要以吉鼎材料為主。吉鼎 CLSM 廠收受再生粒
料，經過混凝土預拌製程，製程控制性低強度回填材料 (CLSM) ，
使用於道路基底層，低密度再生透水混凝土等，將廢棄物產品化，
拓展廢棄物一貫處理流程。吉鼎己於 2Q~3Q24 正式商轉，貢獻
2024 年營收金額 1.03 億元。 2025 年貢獻全年度營運，預拌混凝
土銷售 2025 年營收金額 2.39 億元，YoY+131.6% 。預期 2026 年
第二條產線配合大承再生粒料 4Q26 正式投產，預估預拌混凝土
銷售 2026 年營收金額 3.2 億元， YoY+34.1% 。預期 2027 年在搭
配大承開始投產，帶動預拌混凝土出貨量持續增加，預估預拌混
凝土 2027 年營收金額 4.2 億元， YoY+31.3% 。

太陽能發電主要以大創綠能為主 ， 大創綠能為可寧衛持股 100% 孫
公司。大創綠能主要位於高雄彌陀區設立綠電共生廠，將分三期
規劃，其中第一期裝置容量 69MW ，第二三期裝置容量
53~60MW 。第一期將投入 40 億元建置， 05/2024 開始營運，貢
獻 2024 年營收金額 3.52 億元。在 2025 年貢獻全年度營運，太
陽能發電 2025 年營收金額 5.75 億元， YoY+63.1% 。預期 2026
年，在發電量小幅增加，預估 2026 年太陽能發電營收金額 7.20
億元， YoY+25.3% 。預估 2027 年，在平均單價小幅增加，太陽
能發電營收金額 7.50 億元， YoY+4.20% 。

在其他營收方面， 2026 年將開始貢獻營收，包括高雄 AI 智能高
效焚化爐 BOT 案、大承再生粒料廠。

可寧衛 06/03/2025 公告取得 「高雄市AI 智能高效焚化爐 BOT 案
(小港 )」之最優申請人，可寧衛與台灣人壽成立 SPV 公司，其中
可寧衛持股 55%， 台灣人壽持股45%。 高雄小港焚化爐 ，BOT 案，
舊爐年處理量 34 萬噸，興建新爐年處理量 42 萬噸，營收來源包
括新焚化爐售電收入，新焚化爐代操作與舊焚化爐代操作，其中
新爐將於 2025 年下半年開始興建， 2028~2029 年正式營運，新
爐營運期 20 年。原有焚化爐將有代操作營運，己於 4Q25 正式移
交， 1Q26 開始小幅運作， 2Q26 正式營運，宏遠推估舊爐 2026
年處理量 22~26 萬噸，每噸操作收入 1700~2000 元，宏遠預估小
港焚化爐代操作貢獻 2026 年營收金額 2.5~3 億元（可寧衛持股
55% 進合併營收）。貢獻 2027 年營收金額 3~3.5 億元。

新爐興建方面，在 BOT 會計制度，總興建成本 350 億元，工程期
間 3.5 年，在興建期間需認列工程成本當作營收認列，宏遠推估
新爐 2026 年貢獻營收金額 25 億元， 2027 年貢獻營收金額 40 億
元，上述營收之毛利率為零。

大承再生粒料廠，可寧衛 100% 持股孫公司。大承再生粒料廠主要
使用固化及乾燥製程處理無機污泥 /灰渣 &有機污泥 /無害油泥等製
程再生粒料及資源化燃料，可收受代碼共 35 種（ D 類， R 類， S
類），月處理量 9000 噸，4Q26 末正式營運，預估 2026 年處理量
4.5~6 萬噸，每噸處理費用 6000~8000 元，宏遠預估大承再生粒
料貢獻 2026 年營收金額 0.5~1 億元 。 預估大承再生粒料貢獻2027
年營收金額 5 億元。

可寧衛能源，可寧衛持股 75% 。可寧衛能源主要將在桃園觀音地
區設立固體再生燃料  (SRF) 發電廠，投入 30~35 億元興建， 2022
年底動工。 SRF 發電廠是以回收塑料與廢紙、木材等生質物轉製
成的燃料來發電，再生燃料除了碳排放量大幅低於煤炭外，也能
作為工業鍋爐燃料使用，用途範圍廣泛。可寧衛能源主要營收來
源有兩部分，處理費與售電收入。

可寧衛能源回收塑料與廢紙等生質物，日處理量 350 噸，推估年
處理量 10 萬噸，每噸處理費用 7000 台幣，推估每年營收金額 7
億元。售電收入：再生燃料發電廠裝置容量 17MW ，全年發電度
數 1.2 億度，扣除自用 15% ，每度電 4.74 元計算，年營收金額
4.8 億元 。 宏遠預估2Q~3Q27 正式營運 ， 保守原則先不列入2027
年營收貢獻中。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

預估 2026 年稅後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，稅後 EPS 1.25
元

可寧衛 2026 年雖然在 205 兵工廠整治工程認列金額減少 ， 但2026
年 205 兵工廠營收認列主要以高毛利掩埋比重為主，帶動整體獲
利呈現小幅成長。宏遠預估可寧衛 2026 年營收金額 75.80 億元，
YoY+58.99% ，預估營業利益 20.87 億元， YoY+7.54% ，預估稅
後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.25 元。 (以 1
股分割 10 股後股數計算 )

預估 2027 年稅後盈餘 18.35 億元， YoY+8.24% ，稅後 EPS 1.35
元

可寧衛 2027 年在 205 兵工廠掩埋處理量小幅增加，加上大承再
生粒料廠貢獻全年度營運，預期可寧衛 2027 年營運將持續成長。
宏遠預估可寧衛 2027 年營收金額 96.00 億元， YoY+26.65% ，預
估營業利益 22.97 億元 ，YoY+10.08% ， 預估稅後盈餘18.35 億元 ，
YoY+8.24% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.35 元。 (以 1 股分割 10 股後股數
計算 )

## Document 226: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시_dcb09b7c

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시_dcb09b7c.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 2,000,000원

## Document 282: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Source: Bank of America internal data

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

While some of this spending growth likely reflects the impact of higher prices pushing up spending across a swathe of 
categories, looking just at transactions as a proxy for volumes shows a similar picture, with only slightly less diffusion (Exhibit 5).  
Exhibit 4: Nearly 86% of categories are experiencing positive 
spending growth, accounting for over 94% of retail spending

(monthly, %)

growth, with durables responsible for the declines 
Diffusion index across “more discretionary” retail categories by

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year 
Percentage points contribution to growth by type of services spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 8: …though discretionary spending for the top 5% has eased 
some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.  
Exhibit 9: Necessity spending has gone from a tailwind to a 
headwind across all income cohorts 
Necessity card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 10: Lower-income wage growth remains strongest, despite 
a narrowing gap 
After-tax wage and salary growth by household income tercile (three-
month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories 
Share of spending by the top 5% of households by income across select discretionary categories (three-month moving average to September 2026, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.  
Exhibit 12: It’s early days, but holiday spending is strong so far this year 
Card spending per household for holiday items by year (seven-day moving average, index September 2025 average = 1)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
Note: Holiday items include all merchant category codes for which spending in November through December is at least 20% of total annual spending in the category.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL 
payment in September 
Share of households with a BNPL payment in the month (monthly, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 14: Almost one in five households with BNPL usage has a 
repayment of over $500 
Share of households with BNPL payments by repayment amount 
(monthly, $ amounts)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.

I NSTI TUTE

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, held at –0.03% in September,
unchanged from August.

The Leading Index continues to show moderately improved
momentum compared to the middle of the year, with the
latest reads pointing to growth holding at or slightly below
trend heading into late 2026/early 2027. Growth appears
to be stabilising at a slow pace rather than stalling. That
said, both the latest fuel prices and the RBA’s September
interest rate increase are still working their way through to
the economy with impacts likely to take some of the gloss off

growth near term. Overall, Westpac expects annual growth
to come in at 1.5% for calendar 2026, a below-trend result.
The Leading Index growth rate has firmed slightly over the
last six months, rising from –0.16% back in March to –0.03%
in September. The component detail shows mixed moves over
the period. Four components have contributed more positively
with reduced drags from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Unemployment Expectations Index (adding +0.23ppts to

the Index growth rate); the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Expectations Index (adding another +0.15ppts);
and the S&P/ASX200 (+0.15ppts) combining with a modest
lift in growth in US industrial production (adding +0.12ppts).
These positives have been partially offset by modest drags
from four previously stable components: dwelling approvals
(–0.34ppts); monthly hours worked (–0.08ppts); commodity
prices, measured in AUD terms (–0.07ppts) and the yield

spread (–0.05ppts).
Overall, the component mix speaks to a lack of direction.
Indeed, despite some large shocks over the last year, the
Leading Index growth rate has been relatively contained since
early 2025, holding in a narrow –0.3% to +0.5% range. This
compares to history which, outside of the COVID and GFC
shocks, has seen growth rates move between a much wider
–1.9% to +1.6% range. Of course, the apparent stability,
which is evident across all components of the index, may not

last but for now it suggests the economy has been stuck in a
bit of a holding pattern over the last two years.
Leading Index still tracking a touch below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines
variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken

individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

continue to publish regular bulletin updates through our westpaciq.
com.au website.

## Document 359: Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-11-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-11-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-01

1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
MIDDLE EAST
BULLETIN
Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing

• Under an escalation scenario,

durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards

fuel and LNG prices down faster.

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist

### Page 3

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
attacks on Red Sea shipping keeping Saudi production below
its pre-conflict level until H2 2027. Meanwhile, we look for UAE
production to continue to improve over 2027 and lift above
pre-conflict levels from 2028, as it is no longer constrained by

OPEC quotas. We do not expect a meaningful return of Iranian
oil until an agreement is reached, and damage sustained during
the conflict means production will take time to recover.
We expect global oil demand to be 103.7mb/d in 2026, 0.8mb/d
(0.7%) higher than in 2025, and to rise a further 1.9mb/d (1.8%)
to 105.6mb/d in 2027. Chinese crude imports will continue to
recover over the coming months as refiners restock ahead of
Lunar New Year, but we expect only a gradual return to pre-

conflict levels, as the electrification of transport limits the
recovery in Chinese demand. Globally, demand for diesel and jet
fuel recovers as transport and aviation activity picks up, although
some demand destruction and efficiency gains are expected
to be permanent. The conflict has also highlighted strategic
vulnerabilities, particularly across Asia, so we expect a push to
rebuild reserves adding around 0.5% to demand over 2027.
Fuel prices to ease more slowly than crude

Refined product supply is expected to recover more slowly
than crude, with Gulf refinery runs not returning to pre-war
levels until at least late 2027. Outside the Gulf, there has
been some constructive news. The US has lifted sanctions
on Russian diesel until April 2027, with Russia saying it will
supply more than 4Mt over the coming months, while China
has lifted its ban on refined product exports. However, we
are cautious about how much this will add to the market in

the short term. Much of the Russian supply is headed to the
US and will depend on Russia’s ability to restore refining and
export capacity, while doubts remain over the Russia-Ukraine
energy ceasefire announced by President Trump. China still
manages exports through quotas, which are likely to increase
only gradually as domestic stocks are rebuilt. Refinery runs
should lift ahead of Lunar New Year, but any increase in
exports will take time to reach regional markets.

With product supply recovering slowly and depleted inventories
being rebuilt, Singapore gasoil crack is likely to stay above its
long-run average even as Brent eases, narrowing from US$71/
bbl in Q4 to US$27/bbl by Q4 2027. With Singapore Gas 10ppm
prices, shipping and insurance costs to remain elevated and
easing only gradually in 2027, we forecast Australian diesel
to retail at an average of 274¢/L in Q4, easing to 200¢/L in Q4
2027, and petrol at 230¢/L and 187¢/L respectively.

LNG prices supported through 2027
Japanese LNG prices surged 15.2%mth in September,
averaging US$25.4/MMBtu as supply disruptions in Qatar
and its inability to divert exports around the Strait of Hormuz
tightened markets and intensified competition between
importers. While prices are now more than double pre-conflict
levels, demand destruction across Asia is providing some
offset to tight supply
Our Q4 forecast for Japanese LNG is now US$24.5/mmbtu and

we expect the market to remain structurally tight, with supply
recovery likely to be gradual and prices not returning to pre-
conflict levels until around 2028. Still, Australia’s domestic
gas market should remain insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, which
requires exporters to supply the domestic market with the
equivalent of up to 20% of gas exports. So far this year,
despite the increase in LNG spot prices in Asia, Australian

natural gas prices have remained under the $12 per GJ cap
that has proposed by the Commonwealth government. We are
expecting this to continue well into 2027.
Risks around our oil price outlook are two-sided
Talks are still ongoing in the background, with the US and
Iran exchanging proposals to reopen the Strait, but the gap
between them remains wide. The conflict has also expanded
to the Red Sea and, with inventories sharply drawn down,
there is a smaller buffer to absorb a further shock.

Using the Oxford Economics model, we assess an escalation
scenario, where energy markets face a deeper and more
persistent disruption as the conflict in the Middle East
intensifies. In this case, Brent futures rise to around US$150/
bbl in H1 2027, only dipping to below US$100/bbl in Q2 2028.
We also consider an early resolution scenario, where shipping
and Gulf supply normalise faster, returning Brent to near pre-
conflict levels by end-2027.
Escalation scenario

In the escalation scenario we assume:
Talks stall and the conflict escalates:  the gap between the
US and Iran proves too wide and the US resumes strikes after
the midterms. Iran responds with attacks on shipping and Gulf
energy infrastructure, damaging export, storage and refining
facilities. A lasting agreement is not reached until 2028.
Export routes are disrupted: more frequent attacks curtail
dark crossings of the Strait and ship-to-ship transfers in the

Gulf of Oman. Houthi attacks also cut off Saudi alternative
routes, forcing Gulf producers to cut output and Middle
East crude exports sharply lower. New routes and shipping
arrangements eventually support a recovery in Gulf flows, but
trade patterns take time to adjust.
... and risks remain and while they are two sided ...

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Product supply fails to recover:  The Russia–Ukraine energy
ceasefire does not hold and strikes on Russian refineries
continue, limiting how much diesel Russia can export,
even with the US sanctions waiver extended. China also

reintroduces its export ban to protect domestic supply.
Oil prices lift to around US$150/bbl in H1 2027: With little
buffer to absorb the supply loss, competition for physical
delivery intensifies, with physical prices well above futures
as prompt barrels remain scarce. Higher prices support
supply growth outside OPEC and drive demand destruction,
alongside further emergency stock releases, but a deeper
and more prolonged global deficit remains. Brent stays above

US$100/bbl until mid-2028 as governments rebuild reserves.
The Singapore gasoil crack widens and, with freight and
insurance costs also higher, Australian diesel and petrol prices
average around 247 cpl and 231 cpl at end 2027. Japanese
LNG also remains elevated around US$23.2/MMBtu in Q4
2027, $9.1 above our baseline.
Early resolution scenario
In the early resolution scenario we assume:
A durable agreement: The US and Iran agree terms to reopen
the Strait by the end of year, and, unlike the June interim deal,

the agreement holds, with progress towards a permanent
settlement in early 2027. Attacks on shipping cease and the
US blockade is lifted, allowing Iranian oil back into the market,
initially from storage. Vessels are quickly repositioned to the
Gulf, with freight and war-risk insurance easing from early
2027 rather than mid-year.
A faster recovery in supply:  With export routes secure, Gulf
production and damaged refinery and export capacity recover

sooner than in the baseline. Outside the Gulf, the US extends
its waiver on Russian diesel beyond April 2027 and China
raises export quotas as domestic stocks are rebuilt.
Demand settles below pre-conflict trend: Some demand
destruction and efficiency gains persist, particularly in China.
With inventories rebuilding faster, government restocking is
smaller and more gradual than in the baseline.
Prices fall quickly, but restocking provides a floor:  Brent

Brent futures ease to around US$88/bbl in Q1 2027 as the
premium for prompt physical barrels unwinds. As the market
moves into balance, Brent eases to around US$65/bbl by Q4
2027, close to pre-conflict levels.
Refined product prices ease faster on lower freight costs with
the Singapore gasoil crack narrowing. Australian diesel and
petrol prices fall to around 182 cpl and 170 cpl by Q4 2027. Gas
markets also ease, although damage to Qatar’s gas facility means

export capacity recovers gradually. Japanese LNG falls to around
US$11.9/MMBtu by end-2027, $2.1 below our baseline.
Economic impact
In the escalation scenario, higher fuel costs flow through to
transport, food and other energy-intensive goods such as
plastics and fertilisers. Australian headline CPI remains above
4.5% for much of 2027, with underlying inflation remaining
sticky at above 3% until late 2028. The RBA faces a difficult
trade off between much higher inflation and a significant

hit to activity, as households cut discretionary spending and
businesses defer investment. Higher LNG export earnings
provide only a moderate offset. In the early resolution scenario,
headline inflation is around 0.1ppt below the baseline in 2027.
New Zealand is more exposed in both scenarios, with food
and energy a larger share of its CPI and no offsetting energy
exports. Headline inflation is around 1ppt higher in the
escalation scenario in Q1 2027.

... the upside presents a more dramatic shift in prices

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

Stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

2026 TPCA ： AI 帶動 PCB 全 鏈 規 格 升 級 
AI 規 格 升 級 延 續 ， 材 料 供 給 偏 緊 支 撐 漲 價 
⚫ 高速材料 朝 M9/M10 推進，石英布 需求明確  
⚫ 玻纖布及銅 箔供給偏緊， CCL 漲價延 續至 2027 年 
⚫ NPO 為光通訊 過渡主流， PCB 層數與製 程同步升級  
台光電 (2383) ：公司為 M8 世代首家量產之銅箔基板 (CCL) 供應商，主
要應用於 1.6T 交換器及 AI 伺服器， 2026 年 M9(EM -896) 亦為首家量
產 ， 其中EM-8962 鎖定 ASIC 應用 ， 預計1Q27 量產 。 規格方面 ，EM-892
系列以上屬 M8 等級， K 代表搭配低介電 (Low Dk) 玻纖布，交換器採
用 K2，UBB 因板面積較大改採 K3；K3 及 K4 皆為石英布方案， K4
的 Dk/Df 較前代改善約 50% ，898K4 目前送樣認證中，預計 1Q27 提
供樣品。 M10 送樣中，預計 2026 年完成認證、 2027 年進入 NPI，M10
以 pure 為主 ，M8/M9 則以 hybrid 為主，以兼顧成本與剛性。 PTFE 雖
電性佳，但缺乏玻布支撐，良率與電鍍仍待改善，公 司 不 發 展 PTFE ，
聚焦於既有 HDI 架構下之材料開發。  
載板材料方面， EM-8963 具低熱膨脹係數 (CTE) 特性，已達載板水 準 ，
先導入光模組應用。目前載板材料市場以日韓廠為主，但日韓廠有技
術、缺產能，公司憑藉 HDI 基礎，具良率控制優勢，預期切入速度快。
CPO 方面，公司認為短期 NPO 仍為主流，客戶不會採用缺乏信用紀
錄的材料，公司兼具客戶信任與量產良率； 3.2T scale out 亦有機會採
用 M10。產能方面，公司目前月產能約 600 萬張，預計 2–3 年內翻倍，
2027 年新增之 315 萬張產能全數為 M8 以上高階產品，有助於提高高
階材料占比。  
圖表一、台光電產品線

資料來源：台光電  
德宏 (5475) ： 公司主要產品為石英纖維紗 (Q 紗)及石英纖維布 (Q 布)，
台灣廠目前僅生產 Q 紗，蘇州廠則同時具備 Q 紗及 Q 布產線。台灣 Q
布擴產方面，織布機將於 4Q26 起陸續裝機，預計 3Q27 台灣開始生產
Q 布，初期產能與大陸廠相當，後續依市場狀況逐步增加織布機台數。
織布機自下單到交機需 2–3 年，設備交期為擴產的主要限制。原料方
面，石英純度高 ，原料不會缺貨，一根石英棒可拉出 2–8 萬米纖維。
技 術 方 面 ， 公司 石 英 纖維 專 利 為 自 有， 技 術 路線 與 台 玻(1802) 不同，
拉紗製程需長期累積經驗。另外，因 E 布價格上漲，公司已在中國廠
恢復 E 布生產，因設備仍保留，可較快復產並受惠於 E 布漲價。

### Page 2

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
產評報告

2026/ 10 /27

2 
 客戶認證方面，公司 Q 布切入時間較早， CCL 廠皆已送樣，部分已完成驗證、部分仍在驗證中， Q 紗亦需經終端客戶驗證，目前全球皆有 Q 紗客戶。公司表示產
品皆已可生產，關鍵在客戶是否採購，目前客戶尚未正式下單。獲利結構方面，
Q 布的單價及毛利率皆優於 Q 紗，台灣廠 2027 年開始自產 Q 布後，有助於改善
產品組合及獲利結構。需求方面，以台光電 (2383) 板材為例，通常需使用 4 層以
上 Q 布；公司亦認為 Q 布為低軌衛星的最佳材料方案，散熱性能較佳，應用不限
於 AI 伺服器。  
圖表二 、德宏石英纖維紗

資料來源： 德宏

臻鼎 -KY(4958) ： 公司此次展出 AI 伺服器、高階 HDI 、高速光通訊、低軌衛星及
Physical AI 等 PCB 產品。 AI 主板方面，展出 24 層 N+M 及 34 層厚板 HDI ，採用
M8/M8U 低損耗材料，板厚達 3–5mm 以上，最高電鍍深寬比達 42:1 ；GPU 板方
面，展出 34 層 6 階 HDI，孔徑深寬比 25:1，並採混合材料設計，中間層為電源層，
兼顧訊號傳輸與供電需求，隨層數、階數與板厚同步提升，製程難度及產品價值
亦同步提高。其他應用方面，公司展出 14 層低軌衛星 PCB 、12 層 mSAP 摺疊手
機板，以及 AI PC 、ADAS 、機器人與 AI 眼鏡等產品，應用領域由消費性電子延
伸至車用與 Physical AI 。 
光通訊方面，公司展出 800G、1.6T、XPO 及 NPO 模組板，800G 採 10–12 層，1.6T
及 XPO 採 12–16 層， NPO 達 14–18 層，並導入 mSAP 、埋孔與 Cavity 製程，最
細線寬線距達 25/30μm ，傳輸速率每升一世代，層數及製程精細度皆同步提升。
技術路線方面， CPO 目前主要由輝達 (NVIDIA) 推動，但終端客戶不希望供應鏈集
中於單一供應商， NPO 因此成為折衷方案，並帶動 SLP 類載板需求增加；公司認
為目前真正具量產趨勢的是 NPO ，各技術之間並非互相取代，最終取決於應用場
景。架構方面， 2026 年 CPO 主要應用於 scale out ，scale up 目前仍以可插拔方案
為主，考量功耗與材質，公司預期未來 scale up 用量將達 scale out 的 10 倍以上。

3

資料 來源： 臻鼎

聯茂 (6213) ： 公司重點放在 M9/M10 有布之碳氫 (HC)/PPE 方案， M10 採碳氫樹
脂搭配二代 Q 布，目前送樣中，約需 1.5 年才會放量； M11 亦 以 碳 氫 樹脂 為 主 ，
公司 認為碳氫系列為長期主流。 M9 採碳氫樹脂搭配 Q 布及 HVLP4 銅箔，主要
用於 GPU 大廠加速器 ， 預計 1H27 放量 。 產品結構方面 ，2026 年以 M4/M6 為主，
2027 年主力轉向 M7/M8/M9 ，PCIe Gen6 CPU 所需之 M7 預計 2027 年初放量。
光通訊方面， Low CTE 產品線已送驗 CPO 應用，目前板廠認證中；低軌衛星方
面，1H27 將供應 Meiko 地面站用板， M4 加 M7 合計單月約 30 萬張。產能方面，
集團月產能預計由 2026 年底的 570 萬張提升至 2027 年底 630 萬張、 2028 年底
825 萬張、 2029 年底 915 萬張，擴產以無錫廠及泰國廠為主。

目前公司稼動率約 75%，主要受玻纖布短缺限制，缺料程度依序為 E-glass、Low 
Dk2 、Low Dk1 ，原預期 2H27 舒緩，現看來可能一路偏緊至 2028 年，待陸廠玻
纖布產能開出後，稼動率改善將加速財務表現。漲價方面， 3Q26 漲幅超過 15% ，
之後每季以此幅度為基準持續調漲至 2Q27 ，漲價效應將延續至 2027 年中至下
半年； HVLP4/5 隨需求放大亦將出現短缺，銅箔廠 10 月已開始調價，公司將持
續轉嫁成本。我們認為公司高階產品於 2027 年陸續放量，加上漲價效應延續及
產能持續擴充，營運動能可望延續，投資建議買進，目標價 945 元(2027 年 EPS 
× 18 倍 PER) 。

圖表四 、聯茂高速材料

資料來源：聯茂

華通 (2313) ：公司過去營收主要來自手機相關產品，目前正將產線配置由消費性
產品轉向衛星與資料中心產品 ，非消費性產品目前約占總營收 1/3，2027 年有機
會過半。衛星方面，目前約占營收 2 成，主要客戶正進行火箭升級，衛星亦同
步換代至 V3，2027 年衛星板營收有機會較 2026 年倍增，天上板占衛星營收比
重將提升至 4 成以上，視火箭升級進度，甚至有機會過半。擴產方 面，未來三
年以台灣及泰國為主，台灣大園新廠生產 mSAP 光模組板，觀音廠生產 HDI 及
高層數板 (HLC) ，供 AIDC 加速器及交換器使用；泰國第二廠已完工， 2027 年可
投入量產，第三廠為軟板廠，對應消費性與機器人應用。

光模組方面，插拔式產品採 6 次壓合 HDI ，800G 採 9 次壓合 Any -layer ，2028
年可做到 22 層搭配 mSAP 之 3.2T 產品。網通與資料中心營收於 2Q26 及 3Q26
皆已占集團總營收 10% ，預期 4Q26 光模組明顯增量， 2027 年隨新產能逐季開
出，光模組營收規模將快速成長，網通與資料中心營收規模有機會超越衛星產
品。技術路線方面， CPO 屬載板範疇，公司未投入，並認為 CPO 仍有成本等問
題，過渡期間方案競爭仍多。 AI 加速卡方面，隨運算能力提升，板子層數及階
數同步增加，公司目前可做到 44 層 9 階 HDI ，後續將推進至 56 層 10 階。原料
方面， CCL 供應仍偏緊，漲價可能持續，但隨替代料源陸續獲終端客戶採用，
供應彈性已逐步增加。整體來說，我們正面看待公司後續營運狀況。

《通膨帶動升息壓力，美元走強》
美元從2026年初降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭 、油價
上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐，但此波強勢主要來自地緣
政治風險及利率維持高檔 。從9月Fed季度經濟預測
可見，經濟成長展望維持在 2.5%以下溫和成長 ，通
膨中長期則預期回穩但2026可能繼續維持在3%以上，
因此4Q26美國貨幣政策將繼續有升息的壓力 。從聯
準會點陣圖及 ECB後續政策路徑觀察 ，市場對歐美
利差擴大的預期對美元有利。然而，歐日景氣動能

• 歐元年底目標價維持1.19
3
《歐元投資關鍵-歐元區經濟活動加速擴張》
7月以來，北約示警灰色地帶模糊戰的風險升溫，導
致歐元短線偏弱 ，歐洲資金明顯轉向瑞郎進行避險 。
然而，歐洲基本面此時卻持續改善。歐元區9月景氣
加速擴張 ，綜合PMI初值創41個月新高 ，服務業與
製造業同步改善 ，其中製造業產出創 55個月高點。
德國成長動能增強 ，法國則10個月來首度恢復擴張 。
新訂單連續第3個月成長，增速為2022年5月以來最
快，出口訂單亦持續回升，帶動未完成訂單自2022
年6月以來首度增加。另一方面，中東衝突推升能源

價格，使投入成本與售價增速升至近4個月高點。整
體而言，歐元區經濟韌性優於預期 ，但通膨升溫與
企業信心偏弱仍是後續關注焦點。
貨幣政策方面 ，歐洲央行官員持續釋出偏鷹訊號 ，
認為能源價格再度上漲將推升並延長通膨壓力 ，未
來數月歐元區通膨可能接近4%。愛爾蘭央行行長表
示不排除10月再度升息，強調每次會議皆有行動可
能。首席經濟學家 Lane指出第 2輪能源衝擊將帶動
食品與商品價格上漲 。多位官員認為經濟仍具韌性 ，
為進一步收緊政策提供空間 ，市場也預期未來一年
歐洲央行仍有多次升息機會 ，以防止通膨預期根深
蒂固。基本面繼續支持 ECB升息的環境下 ，預計美
歐利差收斂將提供歐元支撐。4Q26目標價1.19。
